\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(c\left|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\right|\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert\right)\int_{x\in U}\left|\exp\left(\operatorname{i}\frac{\lambda b}{2}x\wedge\xi\right)\left(R_{\lambda b}^{\rm Landau}(z_{\lambda})\widetilde{\psi_{\delta}}\right)(x)\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}{x}\,.

We claim we can choose the set
U
such that both
eq.

C.9
holds and the latter integral over
U
satisfies a lower bound which is independent of
\xi
. We make the following choice:

\displaystyle U:=\Set{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}{C<\left\lVert x-\xi\right\rVert<C^{\prime}}\cap\Set{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}{\operatorname{sgn}(\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\})\ (x\wedge\xi)\geq c\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert}

The set
U
has an area, which is strictly positive and uniformly bounded away from zero, independently of
|\lambda|
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

We then rewrite, via
eq.

C.4
and abusing
K(x-y)\equiv K(\left\lVert x-y\right\rVert)
,

\displaystyle\exp\left(\operatorname{i}\frac{\lambda b}{2}x\wedge\xi\right)\left(R_{\lambda b}^{\rm Landau}(z_{\lambda})\widetilde{\psi_{\delta}}\right)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{y\in K}K_{B,z}(x-y)\operatorname{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\frac{B}{2}x\wedge\left(y-\xi\right)}\widetilde{\psi_{\delta}}(y)\operatorname{d}{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{y\in K}K_{B,z}(x-\xi)\widetilde{\psi_{\delta}}(y)\operatorname{d}{y}+

\displaystyle+\int_{y\in K}\left(K_{B,z}(x-y)\operatorname{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\frac{B}{2}x\wedge\left(y-\xi\right)}-K_{B,z}(x-\xi)\right)\widetilde{\psi_{\delta}}(y)\operatorname{d}{y}

\displaystyle=K_{B,z}(x-\xi)+

\displaystyle+\int_{y\in K}\left(K_{B,z}(x-y)\operatorname{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\frac{B}{2}x\wedge\left(y-\xi\right)}-K_{B,z}(x-\xi)\right)\widetilde{\psi_{\delta}}(y)\operatorname{d}{y}\,.

Now we may establish, via
eq.

C.8
and the lines below it, that

\displaystyle\left|K_{B,z}(\left\lVert x\right\rVert)-K_{B,z}(\left\lVert y\right\rVert)\right|\leq C\left|B\right|\left\lVert x\right\rVert\left\lVert x-y\right\rVert\left|K_{B,z}(\left\lVert x\right\rVert)\right|

for all
\left\lVert x-y\right\rVert\leq c\left|B\right|^{-1}\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-1}
. Using these estimates one may prove

\displaystyle\left|\left(K_{B,z}(\left\lVert x-y\right\rVert)\operatorname{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\frac{B}{2}x\wedge\left(y-\xi\right)}-K_{B,z}(\left\lVert x-\xi\right\rVert)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\beta\left|K_{B,z}(\left\lVert x-\xi\right\rVert)\right|

and hence we have the desired bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert\psi_{\delta}\right\rVert^{2}\gtrsim\exp\left(c\left|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\right|\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert\right)C_{\lambda}

for some constant
C_{\lambda}
independent of
\xi
. By choosing
\xi
sufficiently large we get our result.
∎

Appendix D
Anisotropic Magnetic Harmonic Oscillator

an Appendix by Tal Shpigel

Recall our definition of the magnetic harmonic oscillator (MHO)

\displaystyle h_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}:=\left(P-\frac{1}{2}b\lambda X^{\perp}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}\left\langle X,\left(\nabla\otimes\nabla^{\ast}v\right)(0)X\right\rangle

(D.1)

where
b>0
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
\left(\nabla\otimes\nabla^{\ast}v\right)(0)
is some strictly positive
2\times 2
matrix.

Let
h^{\rm MHO}:=h^{\rm MHO}_{1}
and label its eigenvalues
\Set{e_{j}^{\rm MHO}}_{j=0,1,2,\cdots}
.

In this section, we shall prove

Proposition D.1
.

Set
z_{\lambda}:=-\lambda^{2}+e_{0}^{{\rm MHO}}\lambda+\frac{1}{2}\left(e_{1}^{{\rm MHO}}-e_{0}^{{\rm MHO}}\right)\xi\lambda
where
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
. Hence
z=-\lambda^{2}+\mu\lambda
with

\displaystyle\mu=e_{0}^{{\rm MHO}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(e_{1}^{{\rm MHO}}-e_{0}^{{\rm MHO}}\right)\xi\qquad(\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{1})\,.

This choice guarantees that for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
z_{\lambda}
is at distance order
\lambda
away from the eigenvalues of
h_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
,
\Lambda>0
sufficiently large and consider the wedge

\displaystyle\Omega_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}:=\Set{z\in\mathbb{C}}{\left|z\right|>\Lambda\quad\rm{and}\quad\left|\arg(z)\right|<\frac{\pi}{2}-\varepsilon}\,.

Then there exists some
b_{\rm max}(\varepsilon)>0
such that for all
b\in(0,b_{\rm max}(\varepsilon))
,

\displaystyle(0,\infty)\in\lambda\mapsto r_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}(z_{\lambda})\equiv\left(h_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}-z_{\lambda}\mathds{1}\right)^{-1}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))

continues to an analytic function
\Omega_{\varepsilon}\ni\lambda\mapsto r_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}(z_{\lambda})\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
obeying the following bounds

\displaystyle\left\lVert r_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{MHO}}(z_{\lambda})\right\rVert_{\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))}\lesssim\left|\lambda\right|^{-1}\qquad(\lambda\in\Omega_{\varepsilon})

(D.2)

and

\displaystyle\left\lVert\chi_{S}(X)P_{j}^{\alpha}r_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{MHO}}(z_{\lambda})\chi_{T}(X)\right\rVert_{\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))}\lesssim\exp\left(-c\left|\lambda\right|\mathrm{dist}(S,T)^{2}\right)\qquad(\lambda\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}\land S,T\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}

\displaystyle j=1,2,\alpha=0,1)

which holds as soon as
\mathrm{dist}(S,T)>\left|\lambda\right|^{-1/2}
.

D.1
The Setup

We consider a 2-dimensional anisotropic harmonic oscillator subject to a uniform magnetic field. We start by quoting the Hamiltonian from
[
17
]
:

H=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left(i\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}-\theta_{j}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(k_{1}^{2}x_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}x_{2}^{2}),

(D.4)

where
\theta_{1}=\frac{B}{2}x_{2}
and
\theta_{2}=-\frac{B}{2}x_{1}
. Here,
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
both positive,
B\in\mathbb{R}
and
x=(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. The fundamental solution of the heat equation for this Hamiltonian is given explicitly as
q(t,x,y)
in prop 2.1 of
[
17
]
as

q(x,y,t)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\text{det}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\tilde{S}_{cl}(t,x,y)}{\partial x\partial y}\right)}\exp(-\tilde{S}_{cl}(t,x,y)),

(D.5)

where the exact expression of
\tilde{S}_{cl}(x,y,t)
is given in
[
17
]
.
We will use this to prove bounds on the Green’s function of
H
modified to remove the effect of the ground state. However, there is a small mistake in the expression of the Hamiltonian in (
D.4
). For
q(t,x,y)
as presented in
[
17
]
to be the fundamental solution of the heat equation, the Hamiltonian should have the signs of
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
flipped:
\theta_{1}=-\frac{B}{2}x_{2}
and
\theta_{2}=\frac{B}{2}x_{1}
. We will work with the Hamiltonian with the correct signs.

Next, we perform change of notations and variables to make the expressions more convenient for our goal. The full change of notation is as follows:

\displaystyle k_{i}\to\lambda k_{i},

\displaystyle B\to 2\lambda B,

\displaystyle\lambda t\to s\in\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle q(x,y,t)\to q(x,y,s),

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{cl}(x,y,t)\to\phi(x,y,s),

(D.6)

where, at first,
\lambda
is a large positive number, and
s=\lambda t\in\mathbb{R}
.

After the change of notation and variables, the Hamiltonian becomes

H=\frac{1}{2}\left[-\nabla^{2}+2i\lambda B(x_{2}\partial_{x_{1}}-x_{1}\partial_{x_{2}})+\lambda^{2}\left(k_{1}^{2}+B^{2}\right)x_{1}^{2}+\lambda^{2}\left(k_{2}^{2}+B^{2}\right)x_{2}^{2}\right],

(D.7)

where
\nabla^{2}=\sum_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}
is the laplacian operator and
k_{i=1,2}>0
,
B\in\mathbb{R}
and
x=(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

We look at the heat equation for the Hamiltonian
H
:

(\lambda\partial_{s}+H)q(x,y,s)=0,

(D.8)

where
q(x,y,s)
is the heat kernel. The initial condition is given for
y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that
q(x,y,0)=\delta(x-y)
, where
\delta(x-y)
is the Dirac delta function.

The heat kernel, in this work’s notation and after the change of variables, is given explicitly by

q(x,y,s)=\lambda P(s)\exp(-\phi(x,y,s)),

(D.9)

where the prefactor and the term in the exponent are given by

\displaystyle P(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{K(s)}},

(D.10)

\displaystyle\phi(x,y,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda f_{+}f_{-}\alpha_{12}(s)}{2K(s)}(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})+\frac{\lambda f_{+}f_{-}\beta_{12}(s)}{K(s)}x_{1}y_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda f_{+}f_{-}\alpha_{21}(s)}{2K(s)}(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})+\frac{\lambda f_{+}f_{-}\beta_{21}(s)}{K(s)}x_{2}y_{2}

\displaystyle+i\frac{\lambda B\gamma_{1}(s)}{K(s)}(x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2})-i\frac{\lambda B\gamma_{2}(s)}{K(s)}(x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1}),

(D.11)

and

\displaystyle f_{\pm}=\sqrt{(k_{1}\pm k_{2})^{2}+4B^{2}}

\displaystyle K(s)=f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}(\cosh(f_{+}s)-1)-f_{+}^{2}(k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}(\cosh(f_{-}s)-1),

\displaystyle\alpha_{ij}(s)=k_{i}\big[f_{-}(k_{1}+k_{2})\sinh(f_{+}s)+f_{+}(k_{j}-k_{i})\sinh(f_{-}s)\big],

\displaystyle\beta_{ij}(s)=k_{i}\bigg[\Big(f_{+}(k_{i}-k_{j})+f_{-}(k_{1}+k_{2})\Big)\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}-f_{+}}{2}s\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Big(f_{+}(k_{i}-k_{j})-f_{-}(k_{1}+k_{2})\Big)\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}+f_{+}}{2}s\right)\bigg],

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}(s)=(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})\big(f_{+}^{2}(\cosh(f_{-}s)-1)-f_{-}^{2}(\cosh(f_{+}s)-1)\big),

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}(s)=8f_{+}f_{-}k_{1}k_{2}\sinh\left(\frac{f_{+}}{2}s\right)\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}}{2}s\right).

(D.12)

The ground state eigenfunction of
H
is given by

\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}\eta\zeta}{\pi^{2}}\right)^{1/4}\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda}{2}\left(\eta x_{1}^{2}+\zeta x_{2}^{2}-i\xi x_{1}x_{2}\right)\right),

(D.13)

where

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{+}k_{1}}{k_{1}+k_{2}}

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{+}k_{2}}{k_{1}+k_{2}}

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{2B(k_{1}-k_{2})}{k_{1}+k_{2}}.

(D.14)

The reader should note that there is a small mistake in the expression of the ground state eigenfunction of
H
in
[
17
]
, where the cross term is missing. The eigenvalue corresponding to the ground state is given defined by
H\psi_{0}=E_{0}\psi_{0}
, where
E_{0}
is the ground state energy and given as

E_{0}=\frac{\lambda f_{+}}{2}.

(D.15)

Once we have these formulas we extend the definitions to
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
by analytic continuation, as described in
sections

D.3
and
D.5
.

D.2
Controlled Constants

We are given basic constants
c_{\lambda}
, which will be used to control
|\text{Arg}(\lambda)|
and
k_{1},k_{2}
which specify the harmonic oscillator. Constants that are determined by the basic constants are called controlled constants and are denoted by
c,C,C^{\prime},
etc. These symbols may denote different constants in different occurrences. We write
X=\mathcal{O}(Y)
to indicate that
|X|\leq CY
for a constant
C
.

D.3
First Main theorem

We set
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{C}
with
x\neq y
and
Re(\mu)<\frac{f_{+}}{2}+c_{\mu}
(small enough
c_{\mu}
), where
\Omega=\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}:|\lambda|>C_{\lambda},|\mathrm{arg}(\lambda)|<\frac{\pi}{2}-c_{\lambda}\}
with
C_{\lambda}
large enough. We define the modified Green’s function as

\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda}e^{\mu s}\left[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\right]ds,

(D.16)

where
q(s,x,y)
is the heat kernel defined above in (
D.9
) when all the parameters are real,
E_{0}
is the ground state energy (
D.15
), and
\psi_{0}(x)
is the ground state eigenfunction (
D.13
).
\psi_{0}^{*}
denotes the complex conjugate of
\psi_{0}
. Our purpose is to estimate
\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
.

We also introduce the function:

D(s)=\begin{cases}C\log\left(\frac{C}{s}\right),&\text{if }0<s<1\\
C^{\prime}\exp(-cs),&\text{if }s\geq 1,\end{cases}

(D.17)

with
C,C^{\prime}
large enough and
c
small enough, taken so that
D(s)
is continuous.

Our first main theorem controls the size of
\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
:

Theorem D.2
.

For
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega\times\{\mu\in\mathbb{C}:\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<\frac{f_{+}}{2}+c_{\mu}\}
with
x\neq y
and
|B|
less than a small enough constant
c_{B}
, we have

|\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)|\leq CD(c|\lambda|\ |x-y|(|x|+|y|)).

(D.18)

D.4
Proof of the First Main theorem

We will establish the stronger inequality

\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big|e^{\mu s}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x)\psi_{0}^{*}(y)\big]\Big|ds\leq CD(c|\lambda|\ |x-y|(|x|+|y|)),

(D.19)

for
x\neq y
,
\lambda\in\Omega
, and
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<\frac{\lambda f_{+}}{2}+c_{\mu}
, for small enough
c_{\mu}
.

To prove (
D.19
), we partition the set of all
s>0
into the following six subsets:

Region 1:

0<s<c_{1}
,
s\geq\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}
, and
|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right)s\geq 1
.

Region 2:

0<s<c_{1}
,
s\geq\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}
, and
|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right)s<1
.

Region 3:

0<s<c_{1}
, and
s<\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}
.

Region 4:

c_{1}<s<C_{1}
.

Region 5:

s>C_{1}
, and
|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right)\leq\exp(c^{\sharp}s)
.

Region 6:

s>C_{1}
, and
|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right)>\exp(c^{\sharp}s)
.

We will estimate the integral over each of these regions separately. Here,
c_{1}
and
c^{\sharp}
are small constants, and
C_{1}
is a large constant. These constants will be picked later.

The following props will be useful for the estimates in all regions:

Proposition D.3
.

Suppose
a,b>0
with
ab>c
. Then

\int_{b}^{\infty}\frac{1}{s}\exp(-as)ds\leq C\exp(-ab).

(D.20)

Proposition D.4
.

Suppose
a,b>0
. Then

\int_{0}^{b}\frac{1}{s}\exp(-a/s)ds\leq CD(a/b).

(D.21)

The proofs of these two propositions are left to the reader.

Proposition D.5
.

For
\lambda\in\Omega
,
|B|<c_{B}
, and
\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)
defined as in (
D.13
), we have

\bigg|\frac{1}{\lambda}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\bigg|\leq C\exp(-c|\lambda|s(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})).

(D.22)

Proof.

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{\lambda}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\bigg|=\sqrt{\frac{\eta\zeta}{\pi^{2}}}\Bigg|\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda}{2}(\eta x_{1}^{2}+\zeta x_{2}^{2}-i\xi x_{1}x_{2})-\frac{\lambda}{2}(\eta y_{1}^{2}+\zeta y_{2}^{2}+i\xi y_{1}y_{2})\right)\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\eta\zeta}{\pi^{2}}}\exp\left(-\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}{2}\Big(\eta(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})+\zeta(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})\Big)+\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\xi}{2}\Big(x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\Big(-c\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\big(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\big)-c^{\prime}|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}||B|\big(|x_{1}x_{2}|+|y_{1}y_{2}|\big)\Big).

(D.23)

Now by Cauchy-Schwarz,

|x_{1}x_{2}|+|y_{1}y_{2}|\leq 2\sqrt{(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})}\leq|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}.

(D.24)

Thus, for
|B|<c_{B}
small enough, we have

|\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda)|\leq\exp\left(-c(\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}-|B||\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|)(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)\leq C\exp(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})),

(D.25)

provided
|B|<c_{B}
is small enough.
∎

D.4.1
Regions 1, 2, and 3

The following lem will be useful for the estimates in these regions:

Lemma D.6
.

For
|B|<c_{B}
small enough,
\lambda\in\Omega
, and
0<s<c_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi(x,y,s)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq c|\lambda|s(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+\frac{c|\lambda|}{s}|x-y|^{2},\ \text{and}

\displaystyle|P(s)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{s}.

(D.26)

Proof.

We first move to
w
coordinates defined by

\displaystyle w_{1}

\displaystyle=x_{1}-y_{1},\qquad w_{2}=x_{2}-y_{2},

\displaystyle w_{3}

\displaystyle=x_{1}+y_{1},\qquad w_{4}=x_{2}+y_{2}.

(D.27)

The function
\phi(x,y,s)
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\phi(w,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda f_{+}f_{-}a_{12}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{1}^{2}+\frac{\lambda f_{+}f_{-}a_{21}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda f_{+}f_{-}b_{12}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{3}^{2}+\frac{\lambda f_{+}f_{-}b_{21}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{i\lambda Bg_{-}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{1}w_{4}+\frac{i\lambda Bg_{+}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{2}w_{3},

(D.28)

where
K(s),f_{\pm}
are defined as in (
D.1
) and

\displaystyle a_{ij}(s)

\displaystyle=k_{i}\left[\cosh\left(\frac{f_{+}s}{2}\right)+\cosh\left(\frac{f_{-}s}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left[f_{-}(k_{1}+k_{2})\sinh\left(\frac{f_{+}s}{2}\right)+f_{+}(k_{j}-k_{i})\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}s}{2}\right)\right],

\displaystyle b_{ij}(s)

\displaystyle=k_{i}\left[\cosh\left(\frac{f_{+}s}{2}\right)-\cosh\left(\frac{f_{-}s}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left[f_{-}(k_{1}+k_{2})\sinh\left(\frac{f_{+}s}{2}\right)-f_{+}(k_{j}-k_{i})\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}s}{2}\right)\right],

\displaystyle g_{\pm}(s)

\displaystyle=(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})\left[f_{+}^{2}(\cosh(f_{-}s)-1)-f_{-}^{2}(\cosh(f_{+}s)-1)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\pm 8f_{+}f_{-}k_{1}k_{2}\sinh\left(\frac{f_{+}s}{2}\right)\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}s}{2}\right).

(D.29)

Let us factor out the leading order behavior in small
s
:

\displaystyle a_{ij}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}k_{i}s}{2}\left[\cosh\left(\frac{f_{+}s}{2}\right)+\cosh\left(\frac{f_{-}s}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left[(k_{1}+k_{2})\frac{\sinh(f_{+}s/2)}{f_{+}s/2}+(k_{j}-k_{i})\frac{\sinh(f_{-}s/2)}{f_{-}s/2}\right]=s\cdot\hat{a}_{ij}(s),

\displaystyle b_{ij}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}k_{i}s^{3}}{2}\bigg[\frac{\cosh(f_{+}s/2)-\cosh(f_{-}s/2)}{s^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\bigg[(k_{1}+k_{2})\frac{\sinh(f_{+}s/2)}{f_{+}s/2}+(k_{j}-k_{i})\frac{\sinh(f_{-}s/2)}{f_{-}s/2}\bigg]=s^{3}\cdot\hat{b}_{ij}(s),

\displaystyle g_{\pm}(s)

\displaystyle=f_{+}^{2}f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})s^{2}\bigg[\frac{\cosh(f_{-}s)-1}{f_{-}^{2}s^{2}}-\frac{\cosh(f_{+}s)-1}{f_{+}^{2}s^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\pm 2k_{1}k_{2}\frac{\sinh(f_{+}s/2)}{f_{+}s/2}\frac{\sinh(f_{-}s/2)}{f_{-}s/2}\bigg]=s^{2}\cdot\hat{g}_{\pm}(s),

\displaystyle K(s)

\displaystyle=f_{+}^{2}f_{-}^{2}s^{2}\left[(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}\left(\frac{\cosh(f_{+}s)-1}{f_{+}^{2}s^{2}}\right)-(k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}\left(\frac{\cosh(f_{-}s)-1}{f_{-}^{2}s^{2}}\right)\right]=s^{2}\cdot\hat{K}(s),

(D.30)

and the real part of
\phi
is

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(w,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}f_{+}f_{-}}{2}\left[\frac{a_{12}(s)}{K(s)}w_{1}^{2}+\frac{a_{21}(s)}{K(s)}w_{2}^{2}+\frac{b_{12}(s)}{K(s)}w_{3}^{2}+\frac{b_{21}(s)}{K(s)}w_{4}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}B}{2}\left[\frac{g_{-}(s)}{K(s)}w_{1}w_{4}+\frac{g_{+}(s)}{K(s)}w_{2}w_{3}\right].

(D.31)

First note that
f_{\pm}
are positive constants and
f_{+}>f_{-}
by definition. Also,
\hat{a}_{ij}(s),\hat{b}_{ij}(s)
and
\hat{K}(s)
are positive and finite for small
s>0
. Indeed,
\cosh(f_{+}s/2)\pm\cosh(f_{-}s/2)\geq 0
, and

\displaystyle\frac{\sinh(f_{\pm}s/2)}{f_{\pm}s/2},\ \frac{\cosh(f_{\pm}s/2)-1}{f_{\pm}^{2}s^{2}},\ \text{and }\frac{\cosh(f_{+}s/2)-\cosh(f_{-}s/2)}{s^{2}}\ \text{are finite and positive},

\displaystyle\frac{\sinh(f_{+}s/2)}{f_{+}s/2}\geq\frac{\sinh(f_{-}s/2)}{f_{-}s/2},\qquad\frac{\cosh(f_{+}s)-1}{f_{+}^{2}s^{2}}\geq\frac{\cosh(f_{-}s)-1}{f_{-}^{2}s^{2}},\ \text{and }

\displaystyle k_{1}+k_{2}>k_{i}-k_{j}\ (i,j=1,2),

(D.32)

for all
s\geq 0
, where we define
\sinh(x)/x=1
and
\frac{\cosh(x)-1}{x^{2}}=1/2
for
x=0
.

Therefore,
\hat{a}_{ij}(s),\hat{b}_{ij}(s)
and
\hat{K}(s)
are uniformly continuous in
(s,B)
and positive on the compact set
\{s\in[0,c_{1}]\}\times\{|B|\leq c_{B}\}
and thus attain a positive minimum, so that
a_{ij}(s)/K(s)\geq c/s
and
b_{ij}(s)/K(s)\geq cs
. Similarly,
\hat{g}_{\pm}(s)
is finite for small
s>0
and uniformly continuous in
(s,B)
on
\{s\in[0,c_{1}]\}\times\{|B|\leq c_{B}\}
, so that
|g_{\pm}(s)/K(s)|\leq\hat{C}
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\left[\frac{c}{s}(w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2})+cs(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})\right]-\frac{|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}||B|}{2}\Big[C^{\prime}|w_{1}w_{4}|+C^{\prime}|w_{2}w_{3}|\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{c}\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\left[\frac{w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2}}{s}+s(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})\right]-\hat{C}|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}||B|\Big[|w_{1}w_{4}|+|w_{2}w_{3}|\Big].

(D.33)

Now by Cauchy-Schwarz,

|w_{1}w_{4}|+|w_{2}w_{3}|\leq\sqrt{(w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2})(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})}\leq 2\sqrt{(w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2})(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})},

(D.34)

and for any two positive numbers
N,M
, we have
2\sqrt{NM}\leq N+M
. Thus, for
N=\frac{w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2}}{s}
and
M=s(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})
, we have

|w_{1}w_{4}|+|w_{2}w_{3}|\leq\frac{w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2}}{s}+s(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2}).

(D.35)

Putting everything together, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\tilde{c}\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}-\hat{C}|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}||B|\right)\left[\frac{w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2}}{s}+s(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\geq c^{\prime\prime}|\lambda|\left[\frac{w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2}}{s}+s(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})\right]=c^{\prime\prime}|\lambda|\left[\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{s}+s|x+y|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=c^{\prime\prime}|\lambda|\left[2s(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+\left(\frac{1}{s}-s\right)|x-y|^{2}\right]\geq c|\lambda|\left(s(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{s}\right),

(D.36)

since
\frac{1}{s}-s\geq\frac{1}{2s}
for
0<s<1/2
, so
c_{1}
is chosen small enough. This holds provided
|B|<c_{B}
, for a small enough controlled constant
c_{B}
.

Moving to the prefactor, we saw above that
\hat{K}(s)
attains a positive minimum on
\{s\in[0,c_{1}]\}\times\{|B|\leq c_{B}\}
, so that
|K(s)|\geq c^{\prime\prime}s^{2}
. Remembering
P(s)=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{K(s)}}
, we get
|P(s)|\leq C^{\prime}/s
. The proof of the lem is thus complete.
∎

Region 1

D.6
applies in this region, and moreover

c_{1}\geq s\geq\max\left(\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|},\frac{1}{|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})}\right).

(D.37)

Therefore, in Region 1 we have

\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}q(x,y,s)\bigg|\leq\frac{C}{s}\exp\left(-cs|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right),

(D.38)

and also by
D.5
,

\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[-e^{E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\bigg|\leq C\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right).

(D.39)

These estimates and
D.3
tell us that

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 1}}\Big|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\Big|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\max\left(\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|},\frac{1}{|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})}\right)}^{\infty}\frac{C}{s}\exp\left(-cs|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\cdot\max\left(\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|},\frac{1}{|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\cdot\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}|\lambda||x-y|\cdot(|x|+|y|)\right)\leq C^{\prime\prime}D\left(c|\lambda||x-y|\cdot(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.40)

This controls the integral over Region 1.

Region 2

Again,
D.6
applies in this region, and we have

\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}\leq s\leq\frac{1}{|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})},

(D.41)

hence
|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})s\lesssim 1
, so that

D(\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|))=C\log\left(\frac{C}{|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)}\right).

(D.42)

Also
s<c_{1}
in this region. Then thanks to
D.6
and (
D.41
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 2}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}q(x,y,s)\bigg|ds\leq C\int_{\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}}^{|\lambda|^{-1}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{-1}}\frac{1}{s}ds\leq C\log\left(\frac{|x|+|y|}{\lambda|x-y|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\log\left(\frac{C}{|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)}\right)\leq C^{\prime\prime}D\left(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.43)

Also, using
D.5
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 2}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}[-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})]\bigg|ds\leq C\int_{0}^{c_{1}}\big|\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big|ds

\displaystyle\leq C^{\sharp}\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)\leq C^{\prime\prime}D\left(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.44)

From (
D.4.1
) and (
D.4.1
), we see that

\int_{\text{Region 2}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\bigg|ds\leq CD\left(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.45)

This controls the relevant integral over Region 2.

Region 3

In this region
D.6
applies, and
0<s<\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}
; also
0<s<c_{1}
. So

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 3}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}q(x,y,s)\bigg|ds\leq\int_{0}^{\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}}\frac{C}{s}\exp\left(-\frac{c}{s}|\lambda||x-y|^{2}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq CD\left(\frac{c|\lambda||x-y|^{2}}{\frac{|x-y|}{|x|+|y|}}\right)=CD\left(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.46)

Also, using
D.5
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 3}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}[-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})]\bigg|ds\leq C\big|\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)\leq C^{\prime}D\left(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.47)

From these two estimates, we see that

\int_{\text{Region 3}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\bigg|ds\leq C^{\prime\prime}D\left(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.48)

This controls the relevant integral over Region 3.

D.4.2
Region 4

In this region we have
c_{1}<s<C_{1}
. The following lem will be useful for the estimates in this region:

Lemma D.7
.

For
c_{1}\leq s\leq C_{1}
, and
|B|<c_{B}
small enough, we have

\text{Re}(\phi(x,y,s))\geq c|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right),\quad\text{and}\quad|P(s)|\leq C.

(D.49)

Proof.

We first set
B=0
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{B=0}(w,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda k_{1}}{4}\coth\left(\frac{k_{1}s}{2}\right)w_{1}^{2}+\frac{\lambda k_{2}}{4}\coth\left(\frac{k_{2}s}{2}\right)w_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda k_{1}}{4}\tanh\left(\frac{k_{1}s}{2}\right)w_{3}^{2}+\frac{\lambda k_{2}}{4}\tanh\left(\frac{k_{2}s}{2}\right)w_{4}^{2}.

(D.50)

For
c_{1}<s<C_{1}
,
\coth
and
\tanh
are continuous and bounded from below by a positive constant, so that

\text{Re}(\phi_{B=0}(x,y,s))\geq c^{\prime}|\lambda|\left(w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2}+w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2}\right)=2c^{\prime}|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right).

(D.51)

Now we move to
|B|
small. We remind the reader that
f_{\pm}=\sqrt{(k_{1}\pm k_{2})^{2}+4B^{2}}\to|k_{1}\pm k_{2}|
as
B\to 0
, and
a_{ij},b_{ij},g_{\pm},K
depend on
B
only through
f_{\pm}
. Specifically,

\displaystyle K(s)|_{B=0}

\displaystyle=(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})^{2}\big[\cosh\big((k_{1}+k_{2})s\big)-\cosh\big((k_{1}-k_{2})s\big)\big]

\displaystyle=2(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})^{2}\sinh(k_{1}s)\sinh(k_{2}s)>0,

(D.52)

for
c_{1}\leq s\leq C_{1}
. By uniform continuity in
(s,B)
on the compact set
[c_{1},C_{1}]\times\{|B|\leq c_{B}\}
, there exists
\delta>0
such that whenever
|B|<\delta
, we have, for some
c
,

K(s)\geq c>0,

(D.53)

uniformly for
c_{1}\leq s\leq C_{1}
. We now reduce
c_{B}
so that
c_{B}<\delta
. Hence (
D.53
) holds for
(s,B)\in[c_{1},C_{1}]\times\{|B|\leq c_{B}\}
, so no singularities arise in the denominators. Therefore, we have uniform continuity in
(s,B)
on the compact set
[c_{1},C_{1}]\times\{|B|\leq C_{B}\}
for the following quantities:

\frac{f_{+}f_{-}a_{ij}(s)}{2K(s)},\ \frac{f_{+}f_{-}b_{ij}(s)}{2K(s)},\ \frac{Bg_{\pm}(s)}{2K(s)},

(D.54)

and in particular, they are all uniformly bounded.

Thus, there exists
\tilde{c}>0
such that for
|B|<\tilde{c}
, we have

|\phi(w,s)-\phi_{B=0}(w,s)|\leq\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}|\lambda|(w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2}+w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2}),

(D.55)

so that

\displaystyle\text{Re}(\phi(w,s))

\displaystyle\geq\text{Re}(\phi_{B=0}(w,s))-|\phi(w,s)-\phi_{B=0}(w,s)|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}|\lambda|(w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2}+w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})=c^{\prime}|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}).

(D.56)

We again reduce
c_{B}
so that
c_{B}<\tilde{c}
.

We now move to the prefactor
P(s)
. Since
K(s)
is bounded away from zero for
c_{1}\leq s\leq C_{1}
and
|B|<c_{B}
, we have
|P(s)|\leq C
for some constant
C>0
. This completes the proof of the lem.
∎

We now apply
D.7
, so

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 4}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}q(x,y,s)\bigg|ds\leq C\int_{c_{1}}^{C_{1}}|e^{-\mu s}|\exp(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}))ds

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\exp(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}))\leq C^{\prime}\exp(-c^{\prime\prime}|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|))

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}D(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)).

(D.57)

Moreover by
D.5
,

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 4}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}[-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})]\bigg|ds\leq C\bigg|\frac{1}{\lambda}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)\leq C^{\prime}D\left(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.58)

From (
D.4.2
) and (
D.4.2
), we see that

\int_{\text{Region 4}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\bigg|ds\leq C^{\prime\prime}D\left(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\right).

(D.59)

This controls the relevant integral over Region 4.

D.4.3
Region 5

In this region we have
s>C_{1}
and
|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right)\leq\exp(c^{\sharp}s)
, where
c^{\sharp}
will be picked below. The following lem will be useful for the estimates in this region:

Lemma D.8
.

For
\lambda\in\Omega,\ |B|<c_{B}
and
s>C_{1}
, we write

\displaystyle\phi(x,y,s,\lambda)=\lim_{s\to\infty}\phi(x,y,s,\lambda)+\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)

\displaystyle P(s)=C_{P}\exp(-E_{0}s)\big(1+\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)\big),

(D.60)

where
C_{P}
is the same prefactor that appears in the counterterm
e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})
, and

\displaystyle|\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)|

\displaystyle\leq C\exp(-cs)|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right),\ \text{and}

\displaystyle|\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)|

\displaystyle\leq C\exp(-cs),

(D.61)

for
s\geq C_{1}
large enough, and some constants
C,c>0
. The reader should note that
\phi_{\infty}(x,y,\lambda):=\lim_{s\to\infty}\phi(x,y,s,\lambda)
is the exponent in the ground state
\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})
.

Proof.

For the first estimate, we write the explicit expression for the limit of
\phi(s,x,y)
as
s\to\infty
:

\lim_{s\to\infty}\phi(s,x,y)=\frac{\lambda f_{+}k_{1}}{2(k_{1}+k_{2})}(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})+\frac{\lambda f_{+}k_{2}}{2(k_{1}+k_{2})}(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})-\frac{i\lambda B(k_{1}-k_{2})}{k_{1}+k_{2}}(x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2}).

(D.62)

Then we have

\text{ERROR}_{1}=\lambda[M_{1}(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})+M_{2}x_{1}y_{1}+M_{3}(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})+M_{4}x_{2}y_{2}+M_{5}(x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2})+M_{6}(x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1})]

(D.63)

for
M_{i}=M_{i}(B,k_{1},k_{2})
.

We want to show that

|M_{i}|\leq C\exp(-cs),

(D.64)

for some constants
C,c>0
and large enough
s
. Indeed, if so, then

|\text{ERROR}_{1}|\leq|\lambda|C\exp(-cs)(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}+|x_{1}y_{1}|+|x_{2}y_{2}|+|x_{1}x_{2}|+|y_{1}y_{2}|+|x_{1}y_{2}|+|x_{2}y_{1}|).

(D.65)

By Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

\displaystyle|x_{1}y_{1}|+|x_{2}y_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}}\leq|x|^{2}+|y|^{2},

\displaystyle|x_{1}y_{2}|+|x_{2}y_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}}\leq|x|^{2}+|y|^{2},

\displaystyle|x_{1}x_{2}|+|y_{1}y_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})}\leq|x|^{2}+|y|^{2},

(D.66)

and we get the desired result

|\text{ERROR}_{1}|\leq C^{\prime}|\lambda|\exp(-cs)(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}).

(D.67)

By definition we have
f_{+}>f_{-}>0
. Then

\displaystyle\sinh(f_{+}s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}e^{f_{+}s}\left(1-e^{-2f_{+}s}\right),

\displaystyle|\sinh(f_{-}s)|

\displaystyle\leq e^{f_{-}s}=\frac{1}{2}e^{f_{+}s}\left(2e^{(f_{+}-f_{-})s}\right).

(D.68)

Therefore, for large enough
s
, we have

\alpha_{ij}(s)=k_{i}f_{-}(k_{1}+k_{2})\frac{1}{2}e^{f_{+}s}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right).

(D.69)

Similarly, for large enough
s
, we have

\displaystyle\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}+f_{+}}{2}s\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}e^{f_{+}s}\left[e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}-e^{-(3f_{+}+f_{-})s/2}\right]=e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}\right),

\displaystyle\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}-f_{+}}{2}s\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}e^{f_{+}s}\left[e^{-(3f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}-e^{-(f_{+}+f_{-})s/2}\right]=e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}+f_{-})s/2}\right).

(D.70)

Then,

\beta_{ij}(s)=e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}).

(D.71)

Also,

\displaystyle\cosh(f_{+}s)-1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}e^{f_{+}s}\left(1-e^{-f_{+}s}\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\cosh(f_{-}s)-1

\displaystyle=e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s}).

(D.72)

Then,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}(s)

\displaystyle=(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})\left[f_{+}^{2}e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s}\right)-f_{-}^{2}\frac{1}{2}e^{f_{+}s}\left(1-e^{-f_{+}s}\right)^{2}\right],

\displaystyle=e^{f_{+}s}\left[-\frac{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s}\right)\right],

\displaystyle K(s)

\displaystyle=f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}\frac{1}{2}e^{f_{+}s}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right)-f_{+}^{2}(k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}(e^{(f_{+}-f_{-})s})

\displaystyle=e^{f_{+}s}\left[\frac{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right].

(D.73)

And finally,

\displaystyle\sinh\left(\frac{f_{+}s}{2}\right)\sinh\left(\frac{f_{-}s}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}e^{f_{+}s}\left[e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}-e^{-(f_{+}+f_{-})s/2}-e^{-(3f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}+e^{-(3f_{+}+f_{-})s/2}\right]

\displaystyle=e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}\right),

(D.74)

so that we have

\gamma_{2}(s)=e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}\right).

(D.75)

For the
x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2}
term, we have

\displaystyle|M_{1}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{f_{+}f_{-}\alpha_{12}(s)}{2K(s)}-\frac{f_{+}k_{1}}{2(k_{1}+k_{2})}\bigg|

(D.76)

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2}\frac{k_{1}f_{-}(k_{1}+k_{2})}{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}\frac{1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})}{1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})}-\frac{f_{+}k_{1}}{2(k_{1}+k_{2})}\bigg|

(D.77)

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{f_{+}k_{1}}{2(k_{1}+k_{2})}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right)-\frac{f_{+}k_{1}}{2(k_{1}+k_{2})}\bigg|

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})|\leq C\exp(-cs).

(D.78)

The same result applies for
M_{3}
(the
x_{1}y_{1}
term), since they are defined in the same way as
M_{1}
, but with
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
interchanged.

For the
x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2}
term, we have

\displaystyle|M_{2}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{f_{+}f_{-}\beta_{12}(s)}{K(s)}\bigg|=\Bigg|\frac{f_{+}f_{-}e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})}{e^{f_{+}s}\left[\frac{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right]}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\bigg|\leq C\exp(-cs).

(D.79)

The same result applies for
M_{4}
(the
x_{2}y_{2}
term), since they are defined in the same way as
M_{2}
, but with
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
interchanged.

Next is the cross term
x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2}
. For this case we have

\displaystyle|M_{5}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{iB\gamma_{1}(s)}{K(s)}+\frac{iB(k_{1}-k_{2})}{k_{1}+k_{2}}\bigg|=|B|\Bigg|\frac{e^{f_{+}s}\left[-\frac{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s}\right)\right]}{e^{f_{+}s}\left[\frac{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right]}+\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{k_{1}+k_{2}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=|B|\Bigg|\frac{-f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2})}{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}\frac{1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})}{1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})}+\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{k_{1}+k_{2}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=|B|\Bigg|-\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{k_{1}+k_{2}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right)+\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{k_{1}+k_{2}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=|B||\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})|\leq C\exp(-cs),

(D.80)

provided
|B|
is bounded.

Finally, we have the last cross term
x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1}
. For this case we have

\displaystyle|M_{6}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|-\frac{iB\gamma_{2}(s)}{K(s)}\bigg|=|B|\Bigg|\frac{e^{f_{+}s}\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}\right)}{e^{f_{+}s}\left[\frac{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right]}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=|B|\big|\mathcal{O}\left(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s/2}\right)\big|\leq C\exp(-cs),

(D.81)

again provided
|B|
is bounded. This completes the proof of (
D.64
).

For the prefactor error estimate, we use the above asymptotics for
K(s)
to see that

\displaystyle P(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{K(s)}}=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{e^{f_{+}s}\left[\frac{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right]}}

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{4k_{1}k_{2}}{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}}e^{-f_{+}s/2}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-(f_{+}-f_{-})s})\right)

\displaystyle=C_{P}\exp(-E_{0}s)\big(1+\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)\big),

(D.82)

where one checks the
C_{P}
is the same prefactor that appears in the counterterm

e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*}).

This completes the proof of the lem.
∎

Remember that in region 5 we have
|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\leq\exp(c^{\sharp}s)
. Therefore, by
D.8
and picking
c^{\sharp}
small enough, we have

|\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)|\leq Ce^{-cs}\exp(c^{\sharp}s)\leq C\exp(-c^{\prime}s).

(D.83)

Then

\displaystyle P(s)e^{-\phi(x,y,s)}=C_{P}e^{-E_{0}s}[1+\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)]e^{-\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)-\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)}

\displaystyle=C_{P}e^{-E_{0}s}[1+\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)]e^{-\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)}e^{-\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)}

\displaystyle=C_{P}e^{-E_{0}s}e^{-\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)}\big[1+\text{ERROR}_{3}\big],

(D.84)

with
|\text{ERROR}_{3}|\leq C\exp(-c^{\prime}s)
. On the other hand, we have

\frac{1}{\lambda}e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})=C_{P}e^{-E_{0}s}e^{-\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)}.

(D.85)

We now apply
D.8
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 5}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\bigg|ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\int_{C_{1}}^{\infty}C\big|e^{\mu-E_{0}-c^{\prime}}\big|\bigg|\frac{1}{\lambda}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\bigg|ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C^{\prime}\bigg|\frac{1}{\lambda}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\bigg|,

(D.86)

provided we pick
c_{\mu}
small enough in our hypothesis
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<E_{0}+c_{\mu}
. Continuing with the estimates, and applying
D.5
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 5}}\Big|e^{\mu s}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\Big|ds\leq C^{\prime}\exp(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}D(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)).

(D.87)

This controls the relevant integral over Region 5.

D.4.4
Region 6

In this region we have
s>C_{1}
and
|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right)>\exp(c^{\sharp}s)
. The following lem will be useful for the estimates in this region:

Lemma D.9
.

Let
s>C_{1}
(large enough) and
|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right)>\exp(c^{\sharp}s)
, then

\displaystyle\text{Re}\big(\phi(x,y,s)\big)

\displaystyle\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\geq\frac{1}{2}c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+\frac{1}{2}c\exp(c^{\sharp}s),

\displaystyle|P(s)|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-E_{0}s}.

(D.88)

Proof.

First note that
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{\infty}(x,y,s)\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{\infty}(x,y,s)=\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\big[\eta(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})+\zeta(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})\big]-\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\xi(x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2})

\displaystyle\geq c^{\prime\prime}\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})-|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}||B|C^{\prime}(|x_{1}x_{2}|+|y_{1}y_{2}|)

\displaystyle=c^{\sharp}(\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}-C|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}||B|)(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}),

(D.89)

provided
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|<C^{\prime}|\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|
and
|B|
is small enough (remember
\eta,\zeta>0
). Now, by
D.8
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(x,y,s)=\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{\infty}(x,y,s)+\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\text{ERROR}\right\}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)

\displaystyle\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})-|\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)|

\displaystyle\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\geq\frac{1}{2}c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+\frac{1}{2}c\exp(c^{\sharp}s),

(D.90)

where we used the region 6 hypothesis
|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})>\exp(c^{\sharp}s)
. Picking
c^{\sharp}<c
, we have the desired result.

For the prefactor estimate, we have shown in
D.8
that

P(s)=C_{P}\exp(-E_{0}s)\big(1+\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)\big),\ \text{with}\ |\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)|\leq C^{\prime\prime}\exp(-cs),

(D.91)

and it follows that
|P(s)|\leq Ce^{-E_{0}s}
. The proof of the lem is complete.
∎

We now apply the above
D.9
:

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 6}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}q(x,y,s)\bigg|ds\leq\int_{C_{1}}^{\infty}|e^{(\mu-E_{0})s}|e^{-\frac{c|\lambda|}{2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})}e^{-\frac{c}{2}\exp(c^{\sharp}s)}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{c|\lambda|}{2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)\leq C^{\prime}D\big(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\big).

(D.92)

Moreover, by
D.5
, we have in region 6 that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}[-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})]\bigg|

\displaystyle=\big|e^{(\mu-E_{0})s}\big|\bigg|\frac{1}{\lambda}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq c\big|e^{(\mu-E_{0})s}\big|\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\big|e^{(\mu-E_{0})s}\big|\exp\left(-\frac{c}{2}|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})-\frac{c}{2}\exp(c^{\sharp}s)\right),

(D.93)

so that

\displaystyle\int_{\text{Region 6}}\Big|e^{\mu s}\big[-\frac{e^{-E_{0}s}}{\lambda}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\Big|ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{C_{1}}^{\infty}\big|e^{(\mu-E_{0})s}\big|\exp\left(-\frac{c}{2}\exp(c^{\sharp}s)\right)ds\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{c|\lambda|}{2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}\exp\left(-\frac{c|\lambda|}{2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)\leq C^{\prime\prime}D\big(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\big).

(D.94)

From (
D.4.4
) and (
D.4.4
), we see that

\int_{\text{Region 6}}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\bigg|ds\leq C^{\prime\prime}D\big(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)\big).

(D.95)

This controls the relevant integral over Region 6.

We have controlled the integral over all the regions
1,\cdots,6
of the integral in (
D.18
). All those integrals have been shown to be at most
CD(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|))
. Then (
D.18
) holds. The proof of the first main theorem is now complete.

D.5
Second Main theorem

For the second main theorem, the basic constants are simply
k_{1},k_{2}
and
C_{B}
which will play a role in a moment. As before, controlled constants,
c,c^{\prime}
etc., are constants that depend only on the basic constants. Here we set
\lambda\in\Omega^{\prime}
, where
\Omega^{\prime}=\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}:\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\geq C_{\lambda},|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|\leq c_{\lambda}\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\}
, where
C_{\lambda}
is large enough controlled constant and
c_{\lambda}
is a small enough controlled constant. We now suppose that
B\in\Lambda
, where
\Lambda=\{B\in\mathbb{C}:|\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{B\right\}|\leq C_{B},|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}|\leq c_{B}\}
, for a small enough controlled constant
c_{B}
.

Theorem D.10
.

For
(x,y,\lambda,B,\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega^{\prime}\times\Lambda\times\{\mu\in\mathbb{C}:\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{f\right\}_{+}}{2}+c_{\mu}\}
with
x\neq y
, we have

|\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)|=\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x)\psi_{0}^{*}(y)\big]ds\bigg|\leq CD(c|\lambda|\ |x-y|(|x|+|y|)),

(D.96)

For
\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
defined in (
D.16
), and
D(\cdot)
defined in (
D.17
). Here
C,c,\hat{c}
are controlled constants.

D.6
Proof of the Second Main theorem

The proof follows similar lines to the first main theorem. We will again prove the stronger inequality

\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg|\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]\bigg|ds\leq CD(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|)),

(D.97)

by splitting the integral into the same six regions as in
section

D.4
and showing that the above inequality holds in each region.

D.6.1
Regions 1, 2, and 3

The only work needed here is proving the following lem:

Lemma D.11
.

For
\lambda\in\Omega^{\prime},B\in\Lambda
, and
0<s<c_{1}
small enough, we have

\text{Re}(\phi(x,y,s))\geq c|\lambda|s(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+\frac{c|\lambda|}{s}|x-y|^{2},\quad\text{and }\quad|P(s)|\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{s},

(D.98)

for some controlled constants
c,C^{\prime}>0
.

Proof.

We again move to
w
coordinates. In this case, we write

\displaystyle\phi(w,s)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\bigg[\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{f_{+}f_{-}a_{12}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{1}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{f_{+}f_{-}a_{21}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{f_{+}f_{-}b_{12}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{3}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{f_{+}f_{-}b_{21}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+i\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{Bg_{-}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{1}w_{4}+i\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{Bg_{+}(s)}{2K(s)}w_{2}w_{3}\bigg].

(D.99)

We define the three expressions

\displaystyle\phi_{1ij}(s)=\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{f_{+}f_{-}a_{ij}(s)}{2K(s)},\quad\phi_{2ij}(s)=\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{f_{+}f_{-}b_{ij}(s)}{2K(s)},\quad\phi_{3\pm}(s)=\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{Bg_{\pm}(s)}{2K(s)},

(D.100)

and we will prove estimates for each of these. Starting with
\phi_{1ij}(s)
, we have

\phi_{1ij}(s)=\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2}\frac{\hat{a}_{ij}(s)}{\hat{K}(s)}\frac{1}{s}=\varphi_{1ij}(s,\lambda,B)\frac{1}{s},

(D.101)

where
\hat{a}_{ij},\hat{K}(s)
are defined as in (
D.4.1
). Notice that
\hat{a}_{ij}(s)/\hat{K}(s)
uniformly continuous for
(s,B)
on the compact set
[0,c_{1}]\times\Lambda
. Indeed, we saw in
section

D.4.1
that
\hat{a}_{ij}(s)/\hat{K}(s)
is uniformly continuous for
s\in[0,c_{1}]
. Furthermore, notice that the only dependence on
B
is through
f_{\pm}=\sqrt{(k_{1}\pm k_{2})^{2}+4B^{2}}
. In particular,
\hat{K}(s)
is never zero for any
B\in\Lambda
. Therefore,
\hat{a}_{ij}(s)/\hat{K}(s)
is analytic in
B
and we get the uniform continuity.

Thus, we get that
\varphi_{1ij}(s,\lambda,B)
is uniformly continuous for
(s,\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\},B)\in[0,c_{1}]\times\{\text{The set of }\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\ \text{as in }\eqref{eq:lambda_over_re_lambda}\}\times\Lambda
. Indeed,
f_{+}f_{-}
is analytic in
B
and one can write

\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}=1+i\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}},\quad\text{where }\quad\left|\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\right|\leq c_{\lambda},

(D.102)

because
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|\leq c_{\lambda}|\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|
for
\lambda\in\Omega^{\prime}
, and this is a closed and bounded set.

Next, we see that when
\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0
, we have for
s\in(0,c_{1})
,

\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{1ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}=\phi_{1ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2}\frac{\hat{a}_{ij}(s)}{\hat{K}(s)}\frac{1}{s}\geq\frac{c}{s}>0.

(D.103)

This is because the argument in
section

D.4.1
shows that
\hat{a}_{ij}(s)/\hat{K}(s)\geq c
for
s\in[0,c_{1}]
and
B\in[-C_{B},C_{B}]
. Therefore, by uniform continuity, there exists a
\delta>0
such that whenever
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|<\delta\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}
and
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}|<\delta
we have

|\phi_{1ij}(s)-\phi_{1ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|<\frac{c}{2s},

(D.104)

with
c
as in (
D.103
), and therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{1ij}(s)\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{1ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}-|\phi_{1ij}(s)-\phi_{1ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{s}-\frac{c}{2s}=\frac{c^{\prime}}{s}>0.

(D.105)

We continue to
\phi_{2ij}(s)
:

\phi_{2ij}(s)=\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2}\frac{\hat{b}_{ij}(s)}{\hat{K}(s)}s=\varphi_{2ij}(s,\lambda,B)s,

(D.106)

where
\hat{b}_{ij}(s)
is defined as in (
D.4.1
). By the same arguments as for
\phi_{1ij}(s)
, we see that
\varphi_{2ij}(s,\lambda,B)
is uniformly continuous for

(s,\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\},B)\in[0,c_{1}]\times\{\text{The set of }\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\ \text{as in }\eqref{eq:lambda_over_re_lambda}\}\times\Lambda

. Furthermore, when
\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{2ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}=\phi_{2ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2}\frac{\hat{b}_{ij}(s)}{\hat{K}(s)}s\geq cs>0,

(D.107)

for
s\in(0,c_{1})
, because
\hat{b}_{ij}(s)/\hat{K}(s)\geq c
for
s\in[0,c_{1}]
and
B\in[-C_{B},C_{B}]
. Therefore, by uniform continuity, there exists a
\delta>0
such that whenever
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|<\delta\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}
and
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}|<\delta
we have

|\phi_{2ij}(s)-\phi_{2ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|<\frac{c}{2}s,

(D.108)

with
c
as in (
D.107
), and therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{2ij}(s)\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{2ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}-|\phi_{2ij}(s)-\phi_{2ij}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|

\displaystyle\geq cs-\frac{c}{2}s=c^{\prime}s>0.

(D.109)

Finally, we consider
\phi_{3\pm}(s)
:

\phi_{3\pm}(s)=i\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\frac{B}{2}\frac{\hat{g}_{\pm}(s)}{\hat{K}(s)}=\varphi_{3\pm}(s,\lambda,B),

(D.110)

where
\hat{g}_{\pm}(s)
is defined as in (
D.4.1
). By the same arguments as for
\phi_{1\pm}(s)
, we see that
\varphi_{3\pm}(s,\lambda,B)
is uniformly continuous for

(s,\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\},B)\in[0,c_{1}]\times\{\text{The set of }\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\ \text{as in }\eqref{eq:lambda_over_re_lambda}\}\times\Lambda

. Furthermore, when
\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{3\pm}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}=0,

(D.111)

for
s\in(0,c_{1})
, because
\hat{g}_{\pm}(s)/\hat{K}(s)
is bounded for
s\in[0,c_{1}]
and
B\in[-C_{B},C_{B}]
and everything is real. Therefore, by uniform continuity, for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists a
\delta>0
such that whenever
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|<\delta\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}
and
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}|<\delta
we have

|\phi_{3\pm}(s)-\phi_{3\pm}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|<\varepsilon,

(D.112)

and therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{3\pm}(s)\leq|\phi_{3\pm}(s)|<\varepsilon+|\phi_{3\pm}(s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|=\varepsilon.

(D.113)

Putting everything together, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(w,s)\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\bigg[\frac{c^{\prime}_{1}}{s}(w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2})+c^{\prime}_{2}s(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})-(\varepsilon|w_{1}w_{4}|+\varepsilon|w_{2}w_{3}|)\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq c^{\prime\prime}\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\bigg[\frac{1}{s}(w_{1}^{2}+w_{2}^{2})+s(w_{3}^{2}+w_{4}^{2})\bigg]\geq c|\lambda|s(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+\frac{c|\lambda|}{s}|x-y|^{2},

provided
\varepsilon
is small enough. We now fix
c_{\lambda},c_{B}
smaller than the above
\delta
’s. In moving from the first to the second line, we used the usual Cauchy-Schwarz application (
D.35
). The proof of the first part of the lem is complete.

For the prefactor estimate, we have

P(s)=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{K(s)}}=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{\hat{K}(s)}}\frac{1}{s}=\hat{p}(s,B)\frac{1}{s}.

(D.115)

As seen above,
\hat{K}(s)
is uniformly continuous for
(s,B)\in[0,c_{1}]\times\Lambda
. Therefore,
\hat{p}(s,B)
is uniformly continuous for
(s,B)\in[0,c_{1}]\times\Lambda
. Now for
\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0
, we have

|\hat{p}(s,B)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{\hat{K}(s)}}\leq C,

(D.116)

for
s\in[0,c_{1}]
and
B\in[-C_{B},C_{B}]
from our work in
section

D.4.1
. Therefore, by uniform continuity, there exists a
\delta>0
such that whenever
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}|<\delta
we have

|\hat{p}(s,B)-\hat{p}(s,B)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|<C,

(D.117)

and therefore

|\hat{p}(s,B)|\leq|\hat{p}(s,B)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|+|\hat{p}(s,B)-\hat{p}(s,B)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|\leq C+C=C^{\prime},

(D.118)

for sufficiently small
c_{1},c_{\lambda},c_{B}
(in particular, we reduce
c_{B}
to be less than
\delta
). This completes the proof of the lem.
∎

D.11
allows us to repeat the estimates in
section

D.4.1
exactly, so that we get the desired estimates for regions 1, 2 and 3.

D.6.2
Region 4

Similarly as for regions 1, 2 and 3, a modified version of
D.7
is needed:

Lemma D.12
.

For
\lambda\in\Omega^{\prime},B\in\Lambda
, and
c_{1}\leq s\leq C_{1}
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(x,y,s)\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}),\quad\text{and }\quad|P(s)|\leq C,

(D.119)

for some controlled constants
c,C>0
.

Proof.

Still working in
w
coordinates, we first set
\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0
. Then, from (
D.6.1
) and (
D.100
), we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(w,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}=\lambda[\phi_{112}(s)w_{1}^{2}+\phi_{121}(s)w_{2}^{2}+\phi_{212}(s)w_{3}^{2}+\phi_{221}(s)w_{4}^{2}],

(D.120)

for
s\in[c_{1},C_{1}]
and
B\in[-C_{B},C_{B}]
. From (
D.4.1
), we see that the coefficients

\phi_{112},\phi_{121},\phi_{212},\phi_{221}

are bounded below by a constant
c
. Therefore, for
s\in[c_{1},C_{1}]
and
B\in[-C_{B},C_{B}]
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(w,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}\geq c|\lambda|(|w_{1}|^{2}+|w_{2}|^{2}+|w_{3}|^{2}+|w_{4}|^{2})=c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}).

(D.121)

As seen in the proof of the previous
D.11
, combined with the fact that
s
in this region is bounded away from
0
, we have that the
\phi
’s appearing in (
D.120
) are uniformly continuous for
(s,\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\},B)\in[c_{1},C_{1}]\times\{\text{The set of }\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\ \text{as in }\eqref{eq:lambda_over_re_lambda}\}\times\Lambda
. Therefore, there exists a
\delta>0
such that whenever
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|<\delta\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}
and
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}|<\delta
we have

|\phi(w,s)-\phi(w,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|<\frac{c}{2}|\lambda||w|^{2},

(D.122)

which gives

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(w,s)\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(w,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}-|\phi(w,s)-\phi(w,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|

\displaystyle\geq c|\lambda||w|^{2}-\frac{c}{2}|\lambda||w|^{2}=\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}|\lambda||w|^{2}=c^{\prime}|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}),

(D.123)

for sufficiently small
c_{\lambda},c_{B}
(again, we reduce
c_{\lambda},c_{B}
so they are less than
\delta
). This completes the proof of the first part of the lem.

For the prefactor estimate, again combined with the fact that
s
in this region is bounded away from
0
, we see that
P(s)
is uniformly continuous for
(s,B)\in[c_{1},C_{1}]\times\Lambda
. Indeed, by an argument similar to that in the proof of the
D.11
,
\hat{K}(s)
is uniformly continuous for
(s,B)\in[c_{1},C_{1}]\times\Lambda
and bounded away from zero. More explicitly, we have

P(s)=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{K(s)}}=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{2k_{1}k_{2}}{\hat{K}(s)}}\frac{1}{s},

(D.124)

where
\hat{K}(s)
is defined as in (
D.4.1
). Therefore,
P(s)
is uniformly continuous for
(s,B)\in[c_{1},C_{1}]\times\Lambda
and consequently bounded. This completes the proof of the lem.
∎

With
D.12
, we can now analyze region 4 using the same arguments as in
section

D.4.2
. The estimates go through exactly as before, and we get the desired estimates for region 4.

D.6.3
Region 5

We prove a modified version of
D.8
:

Lemma D.13
.

For
\lambda\in\Omega^{\prime},B\in\Lambda
, and
s>C_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\phi(x,y,s,\lambda)=\lim_{s\to\infty}\phi(x,y,s,\lambda)+\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)

\displaystyle P(s)=C_{P}\exp(-E_{0}s)\big(1+\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)\big),

(D.125)

where

\displaystyle C_{P}

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{+}f_{-}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{4k_{1}k_{2}}{f_{-}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2})^{2}}},

\displaystyle|\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)|

\displaystyle\leq C\exp(-cs)|\lambda|\left(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\right),\ \text{and}

\displaystyle|\text{ERROR}_{2}(s)|

\displaystyle\leq C\exp(-cs),

(D.126)

for some controlled constants
c,C,c^{\prime},C^{\prime}>0
.

Proof.

We start by writing again

\displaystyle\phi(x,y,s)=\lambda

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{f_{+}f_{-}\alpha_{12}(s)}{2K(s)}(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})+\frac{f_{+}f_{-}\beta_{12}(s)}{K(s)}x_{1}y_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{f_{+}f_{-}\alpha_{21}(s)}{2K(s)}(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})+\frac{f_{+}f_{-}\beta_{21}(s)}{K(s)}x_{2}y_{2}

\displaystyle+i\frac{B\gamma_{1}(s)}{K(s)}(x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2})-i\frac{B\gamma_{2}(s)}{K(s)}(x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1})\bigg],

(D.127)

and
\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s):=\lim_{s\to\infty}\phi(x,y,s)
as

\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)=\lambda\bigg[\frac{\eta(B)}{2}(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})+\frac{\zeta(B)}{2}(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})-i\frac{\xi(B)}{2}(x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2})\bigg],

(D.128)

where
\eta(B),\zeta(B),\xi(B)
are defined as in (
D.1
).

With
\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0
, we have
f_{+}>f_{-}>0
. Therefore, by continuity of the square root, we have that for
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}|<\delta
small enough,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{f\right\}_{\pm}>\frac{1}{2}f_{\pm}|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}>0,\quad\text{and likewise}

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{(\right\}f_{+}-f_{-})>\frac{1}{2}(f_{+}|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}-f_{-}|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0})>0.

(D.129)

Thus, for the exponential functions we have

|e^{\pm f_{\pm}s}|=e^{\pm\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{f\right\}_{\pm}s},

(D.130)

and the same arguments goes exactly as in the proof of
D.8
but with the change
f_{\pm}\to\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{f\right\}_{\pm}
. In particular, we get the result for
\text{ERROR}_{1}(x,y,s,\lambda)
.
∎

With
D.13
, we can now analyze region 5 using the same arguments as in
section

D.4.3
. The estimates go through exactly as before, and we get the desired estimates for region 5. Note the corresponding change
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<E_{0}+c_{\mu}
to
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{f\right\}_{+}/2+c_{\mu}
.

D.6.4
Region 6

We prove a modified version of
D.9
:

Lemma D.14
.

For
\lambda\in\Omega^{\prime},B\in\Lambda
, and
s>C_{1}
with
|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})>\exp(c^{\sharp}s)
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}(x,y,s)\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+c\exp(c^{\sharp}s),\quad\text{and }\quad|P(s)|\leq Ce^{-\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{f\right\}_{+}}{2}s},

(D.131)

for some controlled constants
c,C,c^{\prime}>0
.

Proof.

First we show that
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{\infty}(x,y,s)\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})
for some controlled constant
c>0
. For the case where
\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0
, the argument in
D.9
again applies here to show that

\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}).

(D.132)

In particular,
\eta(B),\zeta(B)
are real, positive and bounded by
|B|
, and
\xi(B)
is real and bounded by
|B|
. Also,
|B||\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|<c\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}
where
c
is a small constant. Now write
\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)
as

\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)=\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}{2}\bigg[\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\eta(B)(x_{1}^{2}+y_{1}^{2})+\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\zeta(B)(x_{2}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})-i\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\xi(B)(x_{1}x_{2}-y_{1}y_{2})\bigg],

(D.133)

where
\eta(B),\zeta(B),\xi(B)
are defined as in (
D.1
) and analytic in
B
for
B\in\Lambda
. Therefore, the expressions
\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\eta(B),\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\zeta(B),\frac{\lambda}{\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}}\xi(B)
are uniformly continuous for

(\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\},B)\in\{\text{The set of }\lambda/\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}\ \text{as in }\eqref{eq:lambda_over_re_lambda}\}\times\Lambda.

Then, there exists a
\delta>0
such that whenever
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}|<\delta\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\lambda\right\}
and
|\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}|<\delta
we have

|\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)-\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|<\frac{c|\lambda|}{2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}),

(D.134)

with
c
as in (
D.132
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{\infty}(x,y,s)\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\phi\right\}_{\infty}(x,y,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}-|\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)-\phi_{\infty}(x,y,s)|_{\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{\lambda\right\}=\operatorname{\mathbb{I}\mathbbm{m}}\left\{B\right\}=0}|

\displaystyle\geq c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})-\frac{c|\lambda|}{2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})=\frac{c}{2}|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}),

(D.135)

for sufficiently small
c_{\lambda},c_{B}
. This completes the proof of the first part of the lem.

For the prefactor estimate, we have shown in
D.13
that
|P(s)|=Ce^{-\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{E\right\}_{0}s}(1+\text{ERROR}_{2})
with
|\text{ERROR}_{2}|\leq C\exp(-cs)
for
s>C_{1}
. Then it clearly follows that
|P(s)|\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{E\right\}_{0}s}
for some controlled constant
C^{\prime}>0
. This completes the proof of the lem.
∎

With
D.14
, we can now analyze region 6 using the same arguments as in
section

D.4.4
. The estimates go through exactly as before, and we get the desired estimates for region 6. Note the corresponding change
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<E_{0}+\hat{c}
to
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{E\right\}_{0}+c_{\mu}
.

We have controlled the integral over all the regions
1,\cdots,6
of the integral in (
D.96
). All those integrals have been shown to be at most
CD(c|\lambda||x-y|(|x|+|y|))
. Then (
D.96
) holds. The proof of the second main theorem is now complete.

D.7
Analyticity of the Resolvent

In this section we use the first and second main theorems to prove that the resolvent
\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
is analytic in
\lambda
and
B
in the regions
\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}
and
\Lambda
defined above.

Theorem D.15
.

Let
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)\in\mho
, where
\mho=\{(x,y,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{C}:x\neq y,\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<E_{0}+c_{\mu}\}
for small enough
c_{\mu}>0
. Then the integral

\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\big[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\big]ds

(D.136)

converges for
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)\in\mho
; the function
\tilde{G}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
is continuous on
\mho
and analytic in
(\lambda,\mu)
for fixed
(x,y)
. For
\lambda\in\Omega^{\prime},B\in\Lambda
and with
\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{\mu\right\}<\operatorname{\mathbb{R}\mathbbm{e}}\left\{E\right\}_{0}+c_{\mu}
, the theorem also holds and, moreover,
\tilde{G}(x,y,s,\lambda,\mu)
is analytic in
B
.

Proof.

We first define

G_{N}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)=\int_{1/N}^{N}\frac{e^{\mu s}}{\lambda}\left[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\right]ds,

(D.137)

and will show that
\lim_{N\to\infty}G_{N}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)=G(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
uniformly in
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
varying in any compact subset of
\mho
. This will give the desired result, since
G_{N}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
is analytic in
(\lambda,\mu)
for fixed
(x,y)
and continuous in
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
.

To show the uniform convergence, we need to show that for any
\epsilon>0
and any compact subset
K\subset\mho
, there exists an
N_{0}>0
such that for all
N>N_{0}
and all
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)\in K
we have

\displaystyle|G(x,y,\lambda,\mu)-G_{N}(x,y,\lambda,\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1/N}\Big|e^{\mu s}\left[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\right]\Big|

\displaystyle+\int_{N}^{\infty}\Big|e^{\mu s}\left[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\right]\Big|

\displaystyle<\epsilon.

(D.138)

To prove (
D.7
), we fix
x_{0},y_{0},\lambda_{0},\mu_{0}\in\mho
. Then for small
\delta>0
depending on
x_{0},y_{0},\lambda_{0},\mu_{0}
, we prove both statements:

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to 0}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Big|e^{\mu s}\left[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\right]\Big|ds=0,\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\int_{T}^{\infty}\Big|e^{\mu s}\left[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\right]\Big|ds=0,

(D.139)

uniformly for
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
satisfying

|x-x_{0}|,|y-y_{0}|,|\lambda-\lambda_{0}|,|\mu-\mu_{0}|<\delta.

(D.140)

This will establish and complete the proof of the theorem.

Since
x_{0}\neq y_{0}
for
(x_{0},y_{0},\lambda_{0},\mu_{0})\in\mho
, (
D.140
) implies that

|x-y|>\frac{|x_{0}-y_{0}|}{2}>0,\quad\text{and}\quad|x^{2}|+|y^{2}|<2(|x_{0}|^{2}+|y_{0}|^{2}),

(D.141)

provided
\delta
is small enough in (
D.140
). Therefore, we can use the first and second main theorems, specifically
D.6
and
D.11
, and estimates (
D.39
), (
D.4.1
), (
D.4.1
) to estimate the first integral:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\Big|e^{\mu s}\left[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\right]\Big|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{C}{s}\exp\left(-c|\lambda|\left[s(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})+\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{s}\right]\right)ds+\int_{0}^{\tau}C\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{C}{s}\exp\left(-c|\lambda|\frac{|x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2}}{s}\right)ds+\int_{0}^{\tau}C\exp\left(-c|\lambda|(|x_{0}|^{2}+|y_{0}|^{2})\right)ds\to 0\quad\text{as }\tau\to 0,

(D.142)

uniformly for
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
satisfying (
D.140
).

For the second integral, we use the analysis of regions 5 and 6 in the proof of the first and second main theorems,
D.8
and
D.9
, to prove that

\int_{T}^{\infty}\Big|e^{\mu s}\left[q(x,y,s)-e^{-E_{0}s}\psi_{0}(x,\lambda)\psi_{0}^{*}(y,\lambda^{*})\right]\Big|ds\to 0\quad\text{as }T\to\infty,

(D.143)

uniformly for
(x,y,\lambda,\mu)
satisfying (
D.140
). This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

D.8
The boundedness of the derivative of the resolvent

We want to estimate

r_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{MHO}}(z_{\lambda})f(x_{0})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\partial_{j}\,r_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{MHO}}(z_{\lambda})f(x_{0})

when
\|f\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
,
\operatorname{supp}f\subset B(0,2\Lambda^{-1/2+\eta})
, and

3\Lambda^{-1/2+\eta}\leq|x_{0}|\leq 4\Lambda^{-1/2+\eta}
.

Let
u=r_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{MHO}}(z_{\lambda})f
. From
D.2
we obtain that

|u|\leq\exp(-c\,\Lambda^{\eta})\qquad\text{on }B(x_{0},\Lambda^{-1}).

(1)

In particular,

|u(x_{0})|\leq\exp(-c\,\Lambda^{\eta}).

(2)

To estimate
\nabla u(x_{0})
, note that

\bigl[-\Delta_{x}+\tfrac{\lambda^{2}}{4}|x|^{2}-i\lambda\,x^{\perp}\!\cdot\nabla_{x}+\lambda^{2}v^{\mathrm{MHO}}(x)+E\bigr]u=0\quad\text{on }B(x_{0},\Lambda^{-1}),

(3)

since
f=0
there. (Here
v^{\mathrm{MHO}}(x)=\langle x,Sx\rangle
and
|E|\leq C\Lambda
.)

Rescale from
B(x_{0},\Lambda^{-1})
to the unit disc by setting

\tilde{u}(z)=u\bigl(x_{0}+\Lambda^{-1}z\bigr),\qquad z\in B(0,1).

Then (3) can be rewritten in the form

\Bigl[-\Delta_{z}-i\frac{\lambda}{\Lambda}\bigl(x_{0}^{\perp}+\Lambda^{-1}z^{\perp}\bigr)\!\cdot\nabla_{z}\Bigr]\tilde{u}=-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\Bigl[\tfrac{1}{4}\bigl|x_{0}+\Lambda^{-1}z\bigr|^{2}+v^{\mathrm{MHO}}\!\bigl(x_{0}+\Lambda^{-1}z\bigr)+\tfrac{E}{\lambda^{2}}\Bigr]\tilde{u}\;\equiv\;g(z).

(4)

From (1) we learn that
\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq\exp(-c\,\Lambda^{\eta})
,
while (4) implies (by standard elliptic theory; see e.g.
[
9
,
3
]
) that

|\nabla_{z}\tilde{u}(0)|\leq C\Bigl(\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,1))}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,1))}\Bigr).

Therefore,

|\nabla_{z}\tilde{u}(0)|\leq C^{\prime}\exp(-c^{\prime}\Lambda^{\eta}),

and consequently

|\nabla_{x}u(x_{0})|\leq\exp(-c^{\prime\prime}\Lambda^{\eta}).

(5)

From (1) and (5) we obtain the desired estimates:

\bigl|r_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{MHO}}(z_{\lambda})f(x)\bigr|\;\leq\;\exp(-c\,\Lambda^{\eta})\,\|f\|_{L^{2}},

and

\bigl|\nabla_{x}r_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{MHO}}(z_{\lambda})f(x)\bigr|\;\leq\;\exp(-c\,\Lambda^{\eta})\,\|f\|_{L^{2}},

for
\operatorname{supp}f\subset B(0,2\Lambda^{-1/2+\eta})
and

3\Lambda^{-1/2+\eta}\leq|x|\leq 4\Lambda^{-1/2+\eta}
.

Appendix E
Approximations with the MHO

Consider the Landau Hamiltonian

\displaystyle H^{\mathrm{Landau}}_{\lambda}:=\left(P-\frac{1}{2}b\lambda X^{\perp}\right)^{2}

(E.1)

with
b,\lambda>0
and on it the magnetic harmonic oscillator

\displaystyle h_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}:=H^{\mathrm{Landau}}_{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}\left\langle x,\left(\nabla\otimes\nabla^{\ast}v\right)\left(0\right)x\right\rangle\,.

(E.2)

Here
\left(\nabla\otimes\nabla^{\ast}v\right)\left(0\right)>0
is some
2\times 2
matrix.

Note that by scaling,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{U}_{\sqrt{\lambda}}^{\ast}h_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\mathfrak{U}_{\sqrt{\lambda}}=\lambda h^{\rm MHO}

(E.3)

for a dilation operator
\mathfrak{U}_{\sqrt{\lambda}}
(only unitary if
\lambda
is real) defined in
eq.

A.12
below, and with
h^{\rm MHO}:=h^{\rm MHO}_{1}
. Let us denote the eigenvalues of
h^{\rm MHO}
as
e_{j}^{\rm MHO}
,
j=0,1,\cdots
in ascending order.

We also have our one-well Hamiltonian

\displaystyle h_{\lambda}=H^{\mathrm{Landau}}_{\lambda}+\lambda^{2}v(X)

(E.4)

with
v
having a unique non-degenerate minimum at zero. In particular,
In particular,

\displaystyle v\left(x\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x\to 0}}{{=}}-1+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle x,\left(\nabla\otimes\nabla^{\ast}v\right)\left(0\right)x\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{3}\right)

and where
\left(\nabla\otimes\nabla^{\ast}v\right)
is the Hessian of
v
and we assume
\left(\nabla\otimes\nabla^{\ast}v\right)\left(0\right)>0
.

Then the analysis in
[
18
]
implies that for large
real

\lambda
, if
e_{\lambda,j}
are the eigenvalues of
h_{\lambda}
,

\displaystyle e_{\lambda,j}=-\lambda^{2}+e_{j}^{\rm MHO}\lambda+O\left(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\,.

(E.5)

Even though
[
18
]
does not perform an asymptotic expansion of the eigenvectors, the ideas presented in the non-magnetic
[
22
]
can most likely be generalized to the magnetic case. We don’t need such precision here and present a self-contained proof for the following

Lemma E.1
.

If
\varphi_{\lambda}
is the ground state of
h_{\lambda}
and
\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}
is the ground state of
h_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}
then for all
\lambda
real and sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\mathds{1}-\varphi_{\lambda}\otimes\varphi_{\lambda}^{\ast}\right)\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right\rVert\lesssim\lambda^{-1/2}\,.

(E.6)

Proof.

Through the proof we write
e_{\lambda}
and
e_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}
for the ground state eigenvalues of
h_{\lambda}
and
h_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}
respectively.

Let

\displaystyle v_{\rm err}(x):=v(x)-\left(-1+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle x,\left(\nabla\otimes\nabla^{\ast}v\right)\left(0\right)x\right\rangle\right)\,.

Then as we remarked,
\left|v_{\rm err}(x)\right|=O(\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{3})
as
x\to 0
. Hence

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(h_{\lambda}-\left(-\lambda^{2}+e_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right)\mathds{1}\right)\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right\rVert=\lambda^{2}\left\lVert v_{\rm err}(X)\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right\rVert\,.

(E.7)

We recall the exact ground state of the magnetic harmonic oscillator is of the form

\displaystyle\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}(x):=C\sqrt{\lambda}\exp\left(-\lambda\langle x,Sx\rangle\right)\qquad(x\in\mathbb{R}^{2})

(E.8)

for some
2\times 2
matrix
S>0
, and constant
C<\infty
, both dependent on the Hessian of
v
at the origin. Using this, we can estimate

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}\left\lVert v_{\rm err}(X)\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right\rVert\lesssim\lambda^{1/2}+\operatorname{e}^{-c\lambda}\lesssim\lambda^{1/2}\,.

(E.9)

Now, for any
\psi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
such that
\psi\perp\varphi_{\lambda}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(h_{\lambda}-e_{\lambda}\mathds{1}\right)\psi\right\rVert\geq\frac{1}{\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}\langle\psi,\left(h_{\lambda}-e_{\lambda}\mathds{1}\right)\psi\rangle\geq c_{\rm gap}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert

where, by
eq.

E.5
,
c_{\rm gap}=\left(e_{1}^{\rm MHO}-e_{0}^{\rm MHO}\right)\lambda+O(\lambda^{1/2})
. Thus, if
P:=\varphi_{\lambda}\otimes\varphi_{\lambda}^{\ast}
, then

\displaystyle\left\lVert P^{\perp}\psi\right\rVert\leq\frac{1}{c_{\rm gap}}\left\lVert\left(h_{\lambda}-e_{\lambda}\mathds{1}\right)\psi\right\rVert\qquad(\psi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))\,.

Applying this with
\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}
and using
eq.

E.7
,
eq.

E.9
as well as
eq.

E.5
again, we find

\displaystyle\left\lVert P^{\perp}\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right\rVert\lesssim\lambda^{-1/2}\,.

∎

Next, we deal with the double-well Hamiltonian,

\displaystyle H_{\lambda}\equiv H^{\mathrm{Landau}}_{\lambda}+\lambda^{2}v(X+d)+\lambda^{2}v(X-d)\,.

(E.10)

By the same assumptions on
v
as above and
[
18
]
, if the eigenstates of
H_{\lambda}
are given by
E_{j,\lambda}
, then, a-priori,

\displaystyle E_{0,\lambda}\approx E_{1,\lambda}\approx-\lambda^{2}+e_{0}^{\rm MHO}\lambda+O(\lambda^{1/2})

(E.11)

and the next eigenvalue is at distance
c_{\rm gap}
(as above) away. Let
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}
be the eigenspace of
H_{\lambda}
associated to both eigenvalues
E_{0,\lambda},E_{1,\lambda}
and
\Pi_{\lambda}
the associated self-adjoint projection.

Recall the magnetic translation operators given in
eq.

1.5
. These operators are unitary if
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and otherwise generally not even bounded; in factorized form the two exponentials commute.
\widehat{R}^{z}
commutes with the magnetic kinetic energy
H^{\rm Landau}_{\lambda}
and obeys
eq.

2.46
as well as
eq.

2.47
.

Then we also have

Lemma E.2
.

For all
\lambda
real and sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\lambda}^{\perp}\widehat{R}^{\pm d}\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right\rVert\lesssim\lambda^{-1/2}\,.

(E.12)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left(H_{\lambda}-\left(-\lambda^{2}+e_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right)\mathds{1}\right)\widehat{R}^{d}\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}

\displaystyle=\widehat{R}^{d}\left(h_{\lambda}-\left(-\lambda^{2}+e_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right)\mathds{1}\right)\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}+\widehat{R}^{d}\lambda^{2}v(X-2d)\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}

so by the proof of
eq.

E.9
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(H_{\lambda}-\left(-\lambda^{2}+e_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right)\mathds{1}\right)\widehat{R}^{d}\varphi_{\lambda}^{\rm MHO}\right\rVert\lesssim\lambda^{1/2}+\exp\left(-c\lambda 2d\right)\lesssim\lambda^{1/2}\,.

Otherwise the proof proceeds as in
eq.

E.9
and we get the same result.
∎
